					<div class="vector-body-before-content">
							<div class="mw-indicators">
		<div id="mw-indicator-pp-default" class="mw-indicator"><div class="mw-parser-output"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Protection_policy#semi" title="This article is semi-protected."><img alt="Page semi-protected" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/1b/Semi-protection-shackle.svg/20px-Semi-protection-shackle.svg.png" decoding="async" width="20" height="20" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/1b/Semi-protection-shackle.svg/40px-Semi-protection-shackle.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="512" /></a></span></div></div>

					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-subjectpageheader">
</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr"><p class="mw-empty-elt">


</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Combined_map_-_British_and_Mongol_Empires_a.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A map of the British and Mongol empires at their respective greatest extents" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Combined_map_-_British_and_Mongol_Empires_a.svg/500px-Combined_map_-_British_and_Mongol_Empires_a.svg.png" decoding="async" width="380" height="193" style="--mw-file-upright: 1.5" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Combined_map_-_British_and_Mongol_Empires_a.svg/960px-Combined_map_-_British_and_Mongol_Empires_a.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1002" data-file-height="508" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British Empire</a> (red) and <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol Empire</a> (blue) were the largest and second-largest empires in history, respectively.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The precise extent of either empire at its greatest territorial expansion is a matter of debate among scholars.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Several <a href="/wiki/Empire" title="Empire">empires</a> in <a href="/wiki/Human_history" title="Human history">human history</a> have been contenders for the largest of all time, depending on definition and mode of measurement. Possible ways of measuring size include area, population, economy, and power. Of these, area is the most commonly used because it has a relatively precise definition and can be feasibly measured with some degree of accuracy;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> nevertheless, even area is limited in this regard because of the difficulty in defining the boundaries of empires due to things like the indirect nature of imperial control and sparsely inhabited or even uninhabited areas that may nominally have been controlled by an empire but not meaningfully ruled by it.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Estonian political scientist <a href="/wiki/Rein_Taagepera" title="Rein Taagepera">Rein Taagepera</a>, who published a series of academic articles about the territorial extents of historical empires between 1978 and 1997,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and a book in 2024,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera&#95;&amp;&#95;Nemčok&#95;2024&#95;9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera_&amp;_Nemčok_2024-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> defined an empire in this context as "any relatively large sovereign political entity whose components are not sovereign" and its size as the area over which the empire has some undisputed military and taxation prerogatives.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The list is not exhaustive as there is a lack of available data for several empires; for this reason and because of the inherent uncertainty in the estimates, no rankings are given.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Largest_empires_by_land_area">Largest empires by land area</h2></div>
<p>For context, the land area of the Earth, excluding the continent of <a href="/wiki/Antarctica" title="Antarctica">Antarctica</a>, is 134,740,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (52,023,000&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Empires_at_their_greatest_extent">Empires at their greatest extent</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_(135853015).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853015%29.jpg/250px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853015%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="382" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853015%29.jpg/500px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853015%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6264" data-file-height="9573" /></a><figcaption>The home and colonial areas of the world's empires in 1908, as given by <i>The Harmsworth Atlas and Gazetteer</i></figcaption></figure>
<p>Empire size in this list is defined as the dry land area it controlled at the time, which may differ considerably from the area it claimed. For example: in the year 1800, European powers collectively claimed approximately 20% of the Earth's land surface that they did not effectively control.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Where estimates vary, entries are sorted by the <i>lowest</i> estimate. Where more than one entry has the same area, they are listed alphabetically.

<li id="cite&#95;note-14"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-14">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The Mongol Empire eventually <a href="/wiki/Division_of_the_Mongol_Empire" title="Division of the Mongol Empire">fractured</a> into four separate khanates: the <a href="/wiki/Yuan_dynasty" title="Yuan dynasty">Yuan dynasty</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chagatai_Khanate" title="Chagatai Khanate">Chagatai Khanate</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ilkhanate" title="Ilkhanate">Ilkhanate</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Golden_Horde" title="Golden Horde">Golden Horde</a>. These are listed separately.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Its successor state the <a href="/wiki/USSR" class="mw-redirect" title="USSR">USSR</a> and its successor in turn, the <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russian Federation</a>, reached maximum extents of 22.3&#160;million&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (8.6&#160;million&#160;sq&#160;mi) in 1945 and 17.1&#160;million&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (6.6&#160;million&#160;sq&#160;mi) in 1991, respectively.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera1997&#95;13-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera1997-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-17">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Its successor state the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_China_(1912%E2%80%931949)" title="Republic of China (1912–1949)">Republic of China (1912–1949)</a> and its successor in turn, the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Republic_of_China" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Republic of China">People's Republic of China</a> (since 1949), reached maximum extents of 7.7&#160;million&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (3.0&#160;million&#160;sq&#160;mi) in 1912 and 9.7&#160;million&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (3.7&#160;million&#160;sq&#160;mi) in 1950, respectively.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera1997&#95;13-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera1997-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-20"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-20">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Described explicitly as an empire by Taagepera &amp; Nemčok on p.76<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera2024EngineerEmpires&#95;18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera2024EngineerEmpires-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and Harmsworth on p.27<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Harmsworth&#95;1908&#95;19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Harmsworth_1908-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-PortugalVsBrazil-22"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-PortugalVsBrazil_22-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-PortugalVsBrazil_22-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">The reason the Empire of Brazil is listed as having a larger area in 1889 than the Portuguese Empire had in 1820, despite <a href="/wiki/Colonial_Brazil" title="Colonial Brazil">Brazil having been a Portuguese colony</a>, is that the Portuguese settlers only had effective control over approximately half of Brazil at the time of Brazilian independence in 1822.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera1997&#95;13-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera1997-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-32"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-32">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">More recent reassessment of the historical evidence, both archaeological and textual, has led modern scholars to question previous notions of the extent of the realm of the Medes and even its existence as a unified state.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-MediaAndItsDiscontents&#95;31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MediaAndItsDiscontents-31"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite&#95;note-45"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-45">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The extent to which this constituted a cohesive political entity is uncertain.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Taagepera1978a&#95;41-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Taagepera1978a-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Timeline_of_largest_empires_to_date">Timeline of largest empires to date</h3></div>
<p>The earliest empire which can with certainty be stated to have been larger than all previous empires was that of <a href="/wiki/Upper_and_Lower_Egypt" title="Upper and Lower Egypt">Upper and Lower Egypt</a>, which covered ten times the area of the previous largest civilisation around the year 3000 BC.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305414688" />
</p>

</li>

<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Largest_empires_by_share_of_world_population">Largest empires by share of world population</h2></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_(135853022).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853022%29.jpg/250px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853022%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="384" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853022%29.jpg/500px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_%28135853022%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6229" data-file-height="9574" /></a><figcaption>The home and colonial populations of the world's empires in 1908, as given by <i>The Harmsworth Atlas and Gazetteer</i></figcaption></figure>
<p>Because of the trend of increasing world population over time, absolute population figures are for some purposes less relevant for comparison between different empires than their respective shares of the world population at the time.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For the majority of the time since roughly 400 BC, the two most populous empires' combined share of the world population has been 30–40%. Most of the time, the most populous empire has been located in China.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>46<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>

<li id="cite&#95;note-10"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-10">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" /><cite id="CITEREFTaagepera1979" class="citation journal cs1"><a href="/wiki/Rein_Taagepera" title="Rein Taagepera">Taagepera, Rein</a> (1979). <li id="cite&#95;note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" /><cite class="citation cs2"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20220620115241/https://www.cia.gov/the-world-factbook/countries/world/">"World"</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/The_World_Factbook" title="The World Factbook">The World Factbook</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Central_Intelligence_Agency" title="Central Intelligence Agency">Central Intelligence Agency</a>, 18 July 2022, archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.cia.gov/the-world-factbook/countries/world/">the original</a> on 20 June 2022<span class="reference-accessdate">, retrieved <span class="nowrap">24 July</span> 2022</span>, <q>land: 148.94 million sq km [...] Antarctica 14,200,000 sq km</q></cite><span title="ctx&#95;ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft&#95;val&#95;fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=The+World+Factbook&amp;rft.atitle=World&amp;rft.date=2022-07-18&amp;rft&#95;id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.cia.gov%2Fthe-world-factbook%2Fcountries%2Fworld%2F&amp;rfr&#95;id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AList+of+largest+empires" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite&#95;note-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-12">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" /><cite id="CITEREFMagdoff1979" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Harry_Magdoff" title="Harry Magdoff">Magdoff, Harry</a> (1979). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=A9pWCgAAQBAJ&amp;pg=PA29"><i>Imperialism: From the Colonial Age to the Present</i></a>. NYU Press. p.&#160;29. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-85345-498-4" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-85345-498-4"><bdi>978-0-85345-498-4</bdi></a>. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20200719190026/https://books.google.com/books?id=A9pWCgAAQBAJ&amp;pg=PA29">Archived</a> from the original on 19 July 2020<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">19 July</span> 2020</span>. <q>[I]n 1800 Europe and its possessions, including former colonies, claimed title to about 55 percent of the earth's land surface: Europe, North and South America, most of India, and small sections along the coast of Africa. But much of this was merely claimed; effective control existed over a little less than 35 percent, most of which consisted of Europe itself. By 1878—that is, before the next major wave of European acquisitions began—an additional 6,500,000 square miles (16,800,000 square kilometers) were claimed; during this period, control was consolidated over the new claims and over all the territory claimed in 1800. Hence, from 1800 until 1878, actual European rule (including former colonies in North and South America), increased from 35 to 67 percent of the earth's land surface.</q></cite><span title="ctx&#95;ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft&#95;val&#95;fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Imperialism%3A+From+the+Colonial+Age+to+the+Present&amp;rft.pages=29&amp;rft.pub=NYU+Press&amp;rft.date=1979&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-85345-498-4&amp;rft.aulast=Magdoff&amp;rft.aufirst=Harry&amp;rft&#95;id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DA9pWCgAAQBAJ%26pg%3DPA29&amp;rfr&#95;id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AList+of+largest+empires" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
